\mathbb{P}\!\left(\sqrt{N}\,\|\delta\|_{2}>\varepsilon\,\middle|\,S\right)\;=\;\mathbb{P}\!\left(N\|\delta\|_{2}^{2}>\varepsilon^{2}\,\middle|\,S\right)\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[N\|\delta\|_{2}^{2}\,\middle|\,S\right]\;=\;O\!\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Therefore
\sqrt{N}\,\|\delta\|_{2}\xrightarrow{p}0
.

Combine the two limits with (
99
):

\sqrt{N}\,\max_{1\leq j\leq n}|c_{j}|\;\leq\;\sqrt{N}\,\|\delta\|_{2}+\frac{\sqrt{N}}{n}\;\xrightarrow{p}\;0+0\;=\;0.

A3.

By construction (unpenalized intercept),
\widehat{m}
is linear in
Y_{S}
with weights summing to one,
so
b\equiv 0
.

Variance correction – plug–in variance estimator for SF–PPI.

Recall the notations
Y_{S}=m_{S}+\varepsilon
, where
m_{S}=(m_{0}(X_{1}),\ldots,m_{0}(X_{n}))^{\top}

and
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n})^{\top}
with

\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon\mid X_{S}]=0
and
\Var(\varepsilon\mid X_{S})=\sigma^{2}I_{n}
.
Let
R:=(I_{n}-H)Y_{S}
. We can decompose
\|R\|_{2}^{2}
into three terms

\displaystyle\|R\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|(I_{n}-H)Y_{S}\big\|_{2}^{2}=\big\|(I_{n}-H)(m_{S}+\varepsilon)\big\|_{2}^{2}=\big\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}+(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}+2\,\big\langle(I_{n}-H)m_{S},(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon\big\rangle+\|(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}+2\,m_{S}^{\top}(I_{n}-H)^{\top}(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\top}(I_{n}-H)^{\top}(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}}_{\text{Unscaled bias}}+\underbrace{2\,\varepsilon^{\top}(I_{n}-H)^{2}m_{S}}_{\text{Unscaled cross}}+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{\top}(I_{n}-H)^{2}\varepsilon}_{\text{Unscaled noise}}.

(102)

Recall
A=K+n\lambda I_{n}
and

H\;=\;KA^{-1}(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top})+\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top},\qquad w=\frac{A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}.

Using
\mathrm{tr}(UV)=\mathrm{tr}(VU)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(H)=\mathrm{tr}\!\big(KA^{-1}(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top})\big)+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top})=\mathrm{tr}(KA^{-1})-w^{\top}KA^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}+1.

(103)

Since
K\succeq 0
and
A=K+n\lambda I_{n}
, the eigenvalues of
KA^{-1}
are

\tfrac{\lambda_{i}(K)}{\lambda_{i}(K)+n\lambda}\in[0,1]
. Hence

\displaystyle\|KA^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1,\qquad\mathrm{tr}(KA^{-1})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\lambda_{i}(K)}{\lambda_{i}(K)+n\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{n\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}(K)=\frac{\mathrm{tr}(K)}{n\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda},

(104)

because
0\leq k(x,x)\leq 1
implies
\mathrm{tr}(K)\leq n
.

At this point, we derive a tight upper bound on
\|w\|_{2}^{2}
. Recall that we have

\|w\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{n}}{\big(\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}\big)^{2}}.

Let
z:=A^{-1/2}\mathbf{1}_{n}
. Then

\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{n}=z^{\top}A^{-1}z,\qquad\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}=z^{\top}z,

so

\|w\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{z^{\top}A^{-1}z}{(z^{\top}z)^{2}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}(A^{-1})\,z^{\top}z}{(z^{\top}z)^{2}}=\frac{\lambda_{\max}(A^{-1})}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}.

(105)

By Rayleigh’s inequality,

\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}\;\geq\;\frac{\|\mathbf{1}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}}{\lambda_{\max}(A)}\;=\;\frac{n}{\lambda_{\max}(A)}.

(106)

Using
\lambda_{\max}(A^{-1})=1/\lambda_{\min}(A)
and (
105
)–(
106
),

\|w\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}(A)}{n\,\lambda_{\min}(A)}.

(107)

Since
K\succeq 0
and
k(x_{i},x_{i})\leq 1
,
\lambda_{\max}(K)\leq\operatorname{tr}(K)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k(x_{i},x_{i})\leq n.
Hence

\lambda_{\max}(A)\leq\lambda_{\max}(K)+n\lambda\leq n(1+\lambda),\qquad\lambda_{\min}(A)\geq n\lambda.

Plugging these into (
107
) yields a bound of
\|w\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\|w\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{n(1+\lambda)}{n\cdot n\lambda}=\frac{1+\lambda}{n\lambda}.

(108)

For the mixed term, by Cauchy–Schwarz and
\|\mathbf{1}_{n}\|_{2}=\sqrt{n}
,

\displaystyle\big|\,w^{\top}KA^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}\,\big|\leq\|KA^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|w\|_{2}\,\|\mathbf{1}_{n}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}},

(109)

where we used the bound
\|w\|_{2}^{2}
(
108
). Combining (
103
),
(
104
), and (
109
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(H)\ \leq\ \frac{1}{\lambda}+1+\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}}.\

(110)

Thus, we have
\frac{\mathrm{tr}(H)}{n}\leq\frac{1}{n\lambda}+\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}}
, which implies
\mathrm{tr}(H)=o(n)
since
n\lambda\xrightarrow{}\infty
.

Therefore,

n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\ \geq\ n-O\!\Big(\frac{1}{\lambda}\Big)\ \xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\ \infty\qquad\text{whenever }n\lambda\to\infty.

Set
B:=(I_{n}-H)^{2}
. Since
H
is symmetric,
I_{n}-H
is symmetric, hence
B\succeq 0
and

\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon=\|(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0.

Conditional on
X_{S}
(so
H
and
B
are deterministic), with
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n})^{\top}

i.i.d. mean
0
, variance
\sigma^{2}
, and finite fourth moment, the standard quadratic–form
identities give

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon\,\middle|\,X_{S}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\operatorname{tr}\!\big(B\,\varepsilon\varepsilon^{\top}\big)\,\middle|\,X_{S}\right]=\operatorname{tr}\!\Big(B\,\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon\varepsilon^{\top}\mid X_{S}]\Big)=\operatorname{tr}(B\,\sigma^{2}I_{n})=\sigma^{2}\,\operatorname{tr}(B),

(111)

\displaystyle\Var\!\left(\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon\,\middle|\,X_{S}\right)

\displaystyle=2\sigma^{4}\,\operatorname{tr}(B^{2})\;+\;\kappa_{4}\,\operatorname{tr}(B\circ B)\;\leq\;\big(2\sigma^{4}+|\kappa_{4}|\big)\,\operatorname{tr}(B^{2})\;=\;C\,\operatorname{tr}(B^{2}),

(112)

for a constant
C
depending only on
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{1}^{4}]
. Here,
B\circ B
denotes the Hadamard (entrywise) square of B, and we used
\operatorname{tr}(B\circ B)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}B_{ii}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}B_{ij}^{2}=\|B\|_{F}^{2}=\operatorname{tr}(B^{\top}B)=\operatorname{tr}(B^{2})
since
B
is symmetric.

Next we bound
\mathrm{tr}(B)
and
\mathrm{tr}(B^{2})
in terms of
n
.
Using
A=K+n\lambda I_{n}
and
KA^{-1}=I_{n}-n\lambda A^{-1}
, one can write

\displaystyle I_{n}-H

\displaystyle=I_{n}-\big(KA^{-1}(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top})+\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\big)=\big(I_{n}-KA^{-1}\big)+\big(KA^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(I_{n}-KA^{-1}\big)+\big(KA^{-1}-I_{n}\big)\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}=\big(I_{n}-KA^{-1}\big)\big(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle=n\lambda\,A^{-1}(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}).

Hence

B=(I_{n}-H)^{2}=(n\lambda)^{2}\,(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top})^{\top}A^{-2}(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top})\succeq 0.

From
\|A^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/(n\lambda)
and

\|MN\|_{\mathrm{op}}\;\leq\;\|M\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|N\|_{\mathrm{op}}
(i.e., submultiplicativity of spectral norm), we obtain

\displaystyle\|I_{n}-H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\;

\displaystyle\leq\;n\lambda\,\|A^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\;\leq\;1+\|\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\;=\;1+\sqrt{n}\,\|w\|_{2}\leq 1+\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}}=:C(\lambda),

using
\sqrt{n}\|w\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{(1+\lambda)/\lambda}
proved earlier.

Thus, with
B=(I_{n}-H)^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(B^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}\!\big((I_{n}-H)^{4}\big)=\mathrm{tr}\!\big(((I_{n}-H)^{2})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\big\|(I_{n}-H)^{2}\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\;\mathrm{tr}\!\big((I_{n}-H)^{2}\big)\qquad\text{(since $M\succeq 0\Rightarrow\mathrm{tr}(M^{2})\leq\|M\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathrm{tr}(M)$)}

\displaystyle\leq\|I_{n}-H\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\;\mathrm{tr}(B)\;\leq\;C(\lambda)^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B),

(113)

Moreover,

\mathrm{tr}(B)=\mathrm{tr}\!\big((I_{n}-H)^{2}\big)=n-2\,\mathrm{tr}(H)+\mathrm{tr}(H^{2}).

(114)

We already have
\mathrm{tr}(H)=O(1/\lambda)=o(n)
since
n\lambda\to\infty
(
110
). A convenient bound for
\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})
follows from the decomposition

H\;=\;KA^{-1}+\big(I_{n}-KA^{-1}\big)\,\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}.

Write

Q:=KA^{-1},\qquad T:=(I_{n}-Q)\,\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\quad(\text{rank one matrix}),

so that
H=Q+T
. Then

\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})=\mathrm{tr}(Q^{2})+2\,\mathrm{tr}(QT)+\mathrm{tr}(T^{2}).

Since
A=K+n\lambda I_{n}
is a polynomial in
K
,
K
and
A
commute and share eigenvectors. If
Kv_{i}=\lambda_{i}(K)v_{i}
with
\lambda_{i}(K)\geq 0
, then

Qv_{i}=KA^{-1}v_{i}=\frac{\lambda_{i}(K)}{\lambda_{i}(K)+n\lambda}\,v_{i}=:\mu_{i}v_{i},\qquad\mu_{i}\in[0,1].

Hence

\mathrm{tr}(Q^{2})=\sum_{i}\mu_{i}^{2}\;\leq\;\sum_{i}\mu_{i}=\mathrm{tr}(Q).

Moreover,

\mathrm{tr}(Q)=\sum_{i}\mu_{i}\leq\sum_{i}\frac{\lambda_{i}(K)}{n\lambda}=\frac{\mathrm{tr}(K)}{n\lambda}\leq\frac{n}{n\lambda}=\frac{1}{\lambda},

since
k(X_{i},X_{i})\leq 1
implies
\mathrm{tr}(K)=\sum_{i}K_{ii}\leq n
. Note that, if
n\lambda\to\infty
then
\frac{1}{\lambda}=o(n)
because

\frac{(1/\lambda)}{n}=\frac{1}{n\lambda}\to 0
. Hence, it holds

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(Q^{2})\leq\mathrm{tr}(Q)=\mathrm{tr}(KA^{-1})\leq 1/\lambda=o(n).

(115)

Moreover,

\mathrm{tr}(QT)=\mathrm{tr}\!\big(Q(I_{n}-Q)\,\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\big)=w^{\top}Q(I_{n}-Q)\,\mathbf{1}_{n},

so

\displaystyle|\mathrm{tr}(QT)|

\displaystyle\leq\|Q(I_{n}-Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|w\|_{2}\,\|\mathbf{1}_{n}\|_{2}\leq\Big(\max_{x\in[0,1]}x(1-x)\Big)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{n\lambda}}\cdot\sqrt{n}

(116)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\,\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}}=O\!\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\Big)=o(n).

where the second inequality uses that
Q=KA^{-1}
is symmetric with spectrum in
[0,1]
(hence

\|Q(I_{n}-Q)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\max_{x\in[0,1]}x(1-x)=\tfrac{1}{4}
), together with

\|w\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{n\lambda}}
and
\|\mathbf{1}_{n}\|_{2}=\sqrt{n}
.

Finally, for
T=(I_{n}-Q)\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}
we have

\mathrm{tr}(T^{2})=\big(w^{\top}(I_{n}-Q)\mathbf{1}_{n}\big)^{2}.

Using
I_{n}-Q=I_{n}-KA^{-1}=n\lambda\,A^{-1}
and

w=\dfrac{A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}
,

\displaystyle w^{\top}(I_{n}-Q)\mathbf{1}_{n}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}\right)^{\top}\big(n\lambda\,A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}\big)=n\lambda\,\frac{(A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n})^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}

\displaystyle=n\lambda\,\frac{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{n}}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}\;\leq\;n\lambda\,\lambda_{\max}(A^{-1})\;\leq\;n\lambda\,\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}(A)}\;\leq\;n\lambda\,\frac{1}{n\lambda}\;=\;1,

where the first inequality is a Rayleigh–quotient bound,

\frac{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{n}}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}=\frac{z^{\top}A^{-1}z}{z^{\top}z}\leq\lambda_{\max}(A^{-1})\quad(z:=A^{-1/2}\mathbf{1}_{n}),

the second uses eigenvalue reciprocity for SPD matrices,

\lambda_{\max}(A^{-1})=1/\lambda_{\min}(A)
, and the third uses

\lambda_{\min}(A)\geq n\lambda
since
A=K+n\lambda I_{n}
with
K\succeq 0
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(T^{2})\leq 1=O(1)=o(n).

(117)

Therefore, by summing (
115
), (
116
), and (
117
),

0\leq\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})=\mathrm{tr}(Q^{2})+2\,\mathrm{tr}(QT)+\mathrm{tr}(T^{2})\leq\mathrm{tr}(Q^{2})+2\,|\mathrm{tr}(QT)|+\mathrm{tr}(T^{2})\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}}+1.

Dividing by
n
and using
n\lambda\to\infty
,

\frac{\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})}{n}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda n}+\frac{1}{2n}\sqrt{\frac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}}+\frac{1}{n}\;\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\;0,

hence
\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})=o(n)
.

Recall that
B=(I_{n}-H)^{2}=I_{n}-2H+H^{2},
so taking traces gives
\mathrm{tr}(B)=\mathrm{tr}(I_{n})-2\,\mathrm{tr}(H)+\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})=n-2\,\mathrm{tr}(H)+\mathrm{tr}(H^{2}).
From the bounds already established,
\mathrm{tr}(H)=o(n)
and
\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})=o(n)
, hence

\mathrm{tr}(B)=n+o(n).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}(B)}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}=\frac{n-2\,\mathrm{tr}(H)+\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}=1-\frac{\mathrm{tr}(H)-\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\;\longrightarrow\;1,

(118)

because the second term vanishes due to
\mathrm{tr}(H)-\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})=o(n)
and
n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\sim n
(i.e.,
(n-\mathrm{tr}(H))/n\to 1
).

Our eventual goal is to prove the df–adjusted residual variance is consistent:

\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{\mathrm{KRR}}:=\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)Y_{S}\|_{2}^{2}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\ \xrightarrow{p}\ \sigma^{2}.

Using (
102
) and writing
B:=(I_{n}-H)^{2}
, we divide by
n-\mathrm{tr}(H)
to obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{\mathrm{KRR}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}_{\text{Bias}}\;+\;\underbrace{\frac{2\,\varepsilon^{\top}B\,m_{S}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}_{\text{Cross}}\;+\;\underbrace{\frac{\varepsilon^{\top}B\,\varepsilon}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}_{\text{Noise}}.

(119)

Thus it suffices to show the following three statements:

\displaystyle\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\xrightarrow{p}0,\qquad\text{(Bias vanishes)}

(120)

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{\top}B\,m_{S}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\xrightarrow{p}0,\qquad\text{(Cross vanishes)}

(121)

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{\top}B\,\varepsilon}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\xrightarrow{p}\sigma^{2}.\qquad\text{(Noise converges)}

(122)

We established the following facts, which yield the conclusion:

–

\mathrm{tr}(B)=\mathrm{tr}\!\big((I_{n}-H)^{2}\big)=n-2\,\mathrm{tr}(H)+\mathrm{tr}(H^{2})=n+o(n)
and

\dfrac{\mathrm{tr}(B)}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\to 1
;

–

\mathrm{tr}(B^{2})\leq C(\lambda)^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B)
for a finite constant
C(\lambda)
, and

\|I_{n}-H\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C(\lambda)
;

–

conditional quadratic–form moments:

\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon\mid X_{S}]=\sigma^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B)
and

\Var(\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon\mid X_{S})\leq C\,\mathrm{tr}(B^{2})
for some constant
C
;

–

n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\sim n
as
n\to\infty
(here
a\sim b
denotes asymptotic equivalence, i.e.,
a/b\to 1
; equivalently
\operatorname{tr}(H)=o(n)
).

Variance correction – bias term.

Let
\tilde{m}(x):=s(x)^{\top}m_{S}
be the KRR predictor trained on noiseless labels; then

\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\tilde{m}(X_{j})-m_{0}(X_{j}))^{2}
.
By A1 and the uniform stability of KRR with square loss,
\frac{1}{n}\,\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}\xrightarrow{p}0.
Since
n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\sim n
, it follows that

\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\xrightarrow{p}0,

so (
120
) holds.

Variance correction – noise term.

Using the moment bounds in (
111
)–(
112
), define

Z_{n}\;:=\;\frac{\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon-\sigma^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B)}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}.

Conditioning on
X_{S}
, we have
\mathbb{E}[Z_{n}\mid X_{S}]=0
and

\Var(Z_{n}\mid X_{S})=\frac{\Var(\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon\mid X_{S})}{(n-\mathrm{tr}(H))^{2}}\;\leq\;\frac{C\,\mathrm{tr}(B^{2})}{(n-\mathrm{tr}(H))^{2}}\;\leq\;\frac{C\,C(\lambda)^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B)}{(n-\mathrm{tr}(H))^{2}},

where we used
\mathrm{tr}(B^{2})\leq C(\lambda)^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B)
(
113
). Since
\mathrm{tr}(B)=n+o(n)
and

n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\sim n
, the right-hand side is
O(1/n)\to 0
. By Chebyshev’s inequality, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\left|Z_{n}\right|>\epsilon\,\middle|\,X_{S}\right)\;\leq\;\frac{\Var(Z_{n}\mid X_{S})}{\epsilon^{2}}\;\longrightarrow\;0,

hence

\frac{\varepsilon^{\top}B\varepsilon-\sigma^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B)}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\xrightarrow{p}0.

Moreover,

\frac{\sigma^{2}\,\mathrm{tr}(B)}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}=\sigma^{2}\,\frac{\mathrm{tr}(B)}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\ \longrightarrow\ \sigma^{2},

because
\mathrm{tr}(B)/(n-\mathrm{tr}(H))\to 1
. Combining the two displays yields

\frac{\varepsilon^{\top}(I_{n}-H)^{2}\varepsilon}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\xrightarrow{p}\sigma^{2}.

Variance correction – cross term.

By Cauchy–Schwarz,

\frac{\big|\varepsilon^{\top}Bm_{S}\big|}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\;\leq\;\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}\cdot\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}.

The first factor equals

\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}=\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon^{\top}(I_{n}-H)^{2}\varepsilon}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}},

which converges in probability to
\sigma
by the noise term analysis and the continuous mapping theorem. The second factor equals

\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}=\sqrt{\frac{\|(I_{n}-H)m_{S}\|_{2}^{2}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}}\ \xrightarrow{p}\ 0

by (
120
). Therefore, by Slutsky’s theorem (product of a sequence
converging to a constant and a sequence converging to
0
),

\frac{\big|\varepsilon^{\top}Bm_{S}\big|}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}\ \xrightarrow{p}\ 0,

which proves (
121
).

Variance correction – conclusion.

Combining (
120
)–(
122
) in (
119
) yields

\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{\mathrm{KRR}}\xrightarrow{p}\sigma^{2}
, completing the proof.

B
Algorithms

Algorithm 1: PPI point estimator for general moment equation with a fixed predictor

Input :
Labeled set
S=\{(x_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{n}
, unlabeled covariates
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, predictor
m(\cdot)
, estimating function
U(\theta;x,y)
.

Output :

\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
such that
\widehat{U}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}})=0
.

1

for

j\in S

do

2

compute
\tilde{U}_{j}(\theta)\leftarrow U(\theta;x_{j},m(x_{j}))
;

3

for

j\in S

do

4

compute
U_{j}(\theta)\leftarrow U(\theta;x_{j},y_{j})
;

5

6

Rectifier:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\theta}\leftarrow\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}\!\big[\,U_{j}(\theta)-\tilde{U}_{j}(\theta)\,\big]
;

7

8

Model-based fit:

for

i=1

to

N

do

9

compute
\tilde{U}_{i}(\theta)\leftarrow U(\theta;x_{i},m(x_{i}))
;

10

\displaystyle m_{\theta}\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{U}_{i}(\theta)
;

11

12

Bias-corrected equation:

\widehat{U}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\theta)\leftarrow m_{\theta}+\Delta_{\theta}
;

13

14

Solve:

\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\leftarrow\arg\{\theta:\widehat{U}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\theta)=0\}
;

15

16

return

\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
;

Algorithm 2: 95% Wald confidence intervals for the PPI estimator for general moment equation with a fixed predictor

Input :
Labeled set
S=\{(x_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{n}
, unlabeled covariates
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, predictor
m(\cdot)
, estimating function
U(\theta;x,y)
, and a root
\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
solving
\widehat{U}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}})=0
.

Output :
Componentwise
95\%
Wald confidence intervals for
\theta_{0}
.

1

Jacobian (information) estimate:

\displaystyle\widehat{I}\leftarrow-\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}\partial_{\theta}U\!\big(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}};x_{j},y_{j}\big)
;

2

3

Score-variance part:

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{1}\leftarrow\widehat{I}^{-1}\!\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}U\!\big(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}};x_{j},y_{j}\big)^{\otimes 2}\Big)\widehat{I}^{-1}
;

4

5

Rectifier-variance part:

for

j\in S

do

6

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}})\leftarrow U\!\big(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}};x_{j},y_{j}\big)-U\!\big(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}};x_{j},m(x_{j})\big)
;

7

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{2}\leftarrow\widehat{I}^{-1}\!\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}\Delta_{j}(\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}})^{\otimes 2}\Big)\widehat{I}^{-1}
;

8

9

Asymptotic covariance of
\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Sigma}_{\hat{\theta}}\leftarrow N^{-1}\widehat{V}_{1}\;+\;\big(n^{-1}-N^{-1}\big)\widehat{V}_{2}
;

10

11

95% CIs (componentwise):

for

j=1

to

p

do

12

Output
\displaystyle\Big[\ \hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI},j}\ \pm\ 1.96\,\sqrt{(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\hat{\theta}})_{jj}}\ \Big]
;

13

return

\big\{\,[\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{PPI},j}\pm 1.96\sqrt{(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\hat{\theta}})_{jj}}]\,\big\}_{j=1}^{p}
;

95% Wald confidence intervals for the PPI estimator for general moment equation with a fixed predictor

Algorithm 3: CF-PPI point estimator for general moment equation

Input :
Labeled set
S=\{(x_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{n}
, unlabeled covariates
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, learning algorithm
\mathcal{A}
(maps a labeled subset to a predictor), estimating function
U(\theta;x,y)
, folds
K\geq 2
.

Output :

\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
such that
\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}})=0
.

1

Split (cross–fitting):
Partition
S
into
K
disjoint folds
S_{1},\ldots,S_{K}
.

2

Train
K
out–of–fold predictors:
For each
k\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
, fit
\widehat{m}^{(k)}\leftarrow\mathcal{A}(S\setminus S_{k})
.

3

Unlabeled model–fit (fold average):

\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}U\!\big(\theta;x_{i},\widehat{m}^{\star}(x_{i})\big)\quad\text{such that }\widehat{m}^{\star}(x)=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widehat{m}^{(k)}(x)

4

Labeled rectifier (out–of–fold residuals):

\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\Big[\,U(\theta;x_{i},y_{i})-U\!\big(\theta;x_{i},\widehat{m}^{(k)}(x_{i})\big)\,\Big].

5

Cross–fit score:

\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\theta)\;\leftarrow\;\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}+\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}.

6

Solve:

\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\leftarrow\arg\{\theta:\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\theta)=0\}
;

7

8

return

\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
;

9

10

\ast
Note.
If the estimating equation is linear in its second argument (e.g., mean, GLM scores, etc):

\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}U\!\big(\theta;x_{i},\widehat{m}^{\star}(x_{i})\big)=\frac{1}{KN}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{N}U\!\big(\theta;x_{i},\widehat{m}^{(k)}(x_{i})\big)

Algorithm 3

CF-PPI point estimator for general moment equation

Algorithm 4: CF–PPI point estimator for semiparametric mean estimation

Input :
Labeled set
S=\{(x_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{n}
, unlabeled covariates
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, learning algorithm
\mathcal{A}
(maps a labeled subset to a predictor), folds
K\geq 2
.

Output :

\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
.

1

Split (cross–fitting):
Partition
S
into
K
disjoint folds
S_{1},\ldots,S_{K}
.

2

Train
K
out–of–fold predictors:

for

k\in\{1,\ldots,K\}

do

3

Fit
\widehat{m}^{(k)}\leftarrow\mathcal{A}(S\setminus S_{k})
; for
j\in S_{k}
, set
\widehat{m}^{(-)}(x_{j})\leftarrow\widehat{m}^{(k)}(x_{j})
(out–of–fold).;

4

5

Fold–average predictor (for unlabeled
X
):
Define
\displaystyle\widehat{m}^{\star}(x)\leftarrow\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widehat{m}^{(k)}(x)
.

6

Unlabeled model–based mean:

\displaystyle\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{m}^{\star}(x_{i})
.

7

Rectifier (cross–fit residuals):

\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}\big\{y_{j}-\widehat{m}^{(-)}(x_{j})\big\}\;=\;\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in S_{k}}\big\{y_{j}-\widehat{m}^{(k)}(x_{j})\big\}.

8

Estimator:

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\leftarrow\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}+\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}
.

9

return

\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
;

10

\ast
Note.
The CF–PPI score is

\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}(\theta)=\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}+\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}=(\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}-\theta)+\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}
,
where
\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}:=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{m}^{\star}(x_{i})
,

\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}=\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}-\theta
, and

\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\theta}:=n^{-1}\sum_{j\in S}\{y_{j}-\widehat{m}^{(-)}(x_{j})\}
. In the mean case the rectifier does not depend on
\theta
because

U(\theta;x,y)-U(\theta;x,\widehat{m}(x))=y-\widehat{m}(x)
.

Algorithm 4

CF–PPI point estimator for semiparametric mean estimation

Algorithm 5: 95% Wald confidence interval for the CF-PPI mean estimator for semiparametric mean estimation

Input :
Labeled set
S=\{(x_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{n}
, unlabeled covariates
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, fold–average predictor
\widehat{m}^{\star}(\cdot)
and out-of-fold (O.O.F.) labeled predictions
\widehat{m}^{(-)}(x_{j})
from Algorithm
4
; estimator
\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}=\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}+\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}
with

\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{m}^{\star}(x_{i})=\big(KN\big)^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{m}^{(k)}(x_{i})

and
\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}=n^{-1}\sum_{j\in S}\{y_{j}-\widehat{m}^{(-)}(x_{j})\}
.

Output :
A 95% Wald confidence interval for
\theta_{0}=\mathbb{E}[Y]
.

1

Unlabeled variance (model–fit term):

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{U}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{1}{N}\,\widehat{\Var}\!\big(\widehat{m}^{\star}(X)\big)\;=\;\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\big(\widehat{m}^{\star}(x_{i})-\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{cf}}\big)^{2}.

2

Rectifier variance (out-of-fold (O.O.F.) residuals):

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{S}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{1}{n}\,\widehat{\Var}\!\big(Y-\widehat{m}^{(-)}(X)\big)\;=\;\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{j\in S}\Big(\,y_{j}-\widehat{m}^{(-)}(x_{j})-\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{cf}}\,\Big)^{2}.

3

Asymptotic variance:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Var}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\big)\;\leftarrow\;\widehat{V}_{U}+\widehat{V}_{S}.

4

Standard error:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{se}}\;\leftarrow\;\sqrt{\widehat{\Var}\!\big(\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\big)}.

5

return (95% Wald CI):

\displaystyle\Big[\,\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\ \pm\ 1.96\,\widehat{\mathrm{se}}\,\Big]
.

6

\ast
Note.
The variance estimator equals

\widehat{\Var}(\widehat{m}^{\star}(X))/N+\widehat{\Var}(Y-\widehat{m}^{(-)}(X))/n
.

Algorithm 5

95% Wald confidence interval for
\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{cf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
for semiparametric mean estimation

Algorithm 6: SF–PPI point estimator for semiparametric mean estimation

Input :
Labeled set
S=\{(x_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{n}
, unlabeled covariates
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, learning algorithm
\mathcal{A}
(maps a labeled set to a predictor).

Output :

\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
.

1

Fit single predictor:

\widehat{m}\leftarrow\mathcal{A}(S)
.

2

Unlabeled model–based mean:

\displaystyle\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{sf}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{m}(x_{i})
.

3

Rectifier (in–sample residuals):

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{sf}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}\!\big\{\,y_{j}-\widehat{m}(x_{j})\,\big\}
.

4

Estimator (closed form):

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\leftarrow\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{sf}}+\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{sf}}
.

5

return

\hat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
.

6

\ast
Note.
The SF–PPI point estimator is the vanilla PPI plug–in with a single fitted predictor: the model is trained once on the labeled set
S
and reused both to impute
\widehat{m}(x_{i})
for the unlabeled covariates and to compute the in–sample labeled residuals
y_{j}-\widehat{m}(x_{j})
. No cross–fitting is performed; the “VC’’ in SF-PPI-VC only affects variance/CI, not the point estimate.

7

\ast
Note.
For the learning algorithm, we adopt kernel ridge regression (Gaussian kernel) with an unpenalized intercept; any supervised learner may be substituted.

Algorithm 6

SF–PPI point estimator for semiparametric mean estimation

Algorithm 7: 95% Wald confidence interval for the SF–PPI estimator with variance correction

Input :
Labeled set
S=\{(x_{j},y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{n}
, unlabeled covariates
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, Gaussian kernel
k_{\ell}(x,x^{\prime})=\exp\!\big(-(x-x^{\prime})^{2}/(2\ell^{2})\big)
, ridge penalty
\lambda>0
.

Output :
A 95% Wald confidence interval for
\theta_{0}=\mathbb{E}[Y]
.

1

KRR with unpenalized intercept.

Compute the Gram matrix
K\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
,
K_{jj^{\prime}}=k_{\ell}(x_{j},x_{j^{\prime}})
; set

A\leftarrow K+n\lambda I_{n}
,
\mathbf{1}_{n}=(1,\ldots,1)^{\top}
, and

w^{\top}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}}{\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}A^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n}}\,.

2

Hat matrix on labeled points (
n\times n
):

H\;\leftarrow\;KA^{-1}\big(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\big)+\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}.

3

Unlabeled weights (
s(x_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
):

s(x_{i})^{\top}\;\leftarrow\;k(x_{i},X_{S})^{\top}A^{-1}\big(I_{n}-\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}\big)+\mathbf{1}_{n}w^{\top}.

4

Weight matrix (
N\times n
):

S_{U}\;=\;\begin{bmatrix}s(x_{1})^{\top}\\[-2.0pt]
\vdots\\[-2.0pt]
s(x_{N})^{\top}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}.

5

Predictions:

\widehat{m}_{S}\leftarrow HY_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\qquad\widehat{m}_{U}\leftarrow S_{U}Y_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

6

SF–PPI point estimate:

\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{sf}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{m}_{U}[i],\qquad\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{sf}}\leftarrow\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in S}\!\big(y_{j}-\widehat{m}_{S}[j]\big),\qquad\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\leftarrow\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{sf}}+\widehat{\Delta}^{\mathrm{sf}}.

7

Residual variance (df–corrected):

\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{\mathrm{KRR}}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{\|Y_{S}-\widehat{m}_{S}\|_{2}^{2}}{\,n-\mathrm{tr}(H)\,}.

8

Unlabeled signal variance:

s_{U}^{2}\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|s(x_{i})\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{N}\|S_{U}\|_{F}^{2},\qquad\widehat{\Var}(\widehat{m}_{U})\leftarrow\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\big(\widehat{m}_{U}[i]-\widehat{m}^{\mathrm{sf}}\big)^{2},

\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{signal}}\;\leftarrow\;\frac{\widehat{\Var}(\widehat{m}_{U})-\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{\mathrm{KRR}}\,s_{U}^{2}}{N}.

9

Labeled noise (variance–correction) term:

a\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}S_{U}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\qquad b\leftarrow\frac{1}{n}\big(I_{n}-H^{\top}\big)\mathbf{1}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\qquad c\leftarrow a+b,\qquad\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{VC}}\leftarrow\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{\mathrm{KRR}}\,\|c\|_{2}^{2}.

10

Asymptotic variance and SE:
;

11

\widehat{\Var}\!\big(\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\big)\leftarrow\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{signal}}+\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{VC}},\qquad\widehat{\mathrm{se}}\leftarrow\sqrt{\widehat{\Var}\!\big(\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\big)}.

12

Return (95% Wald CI):

\quad\Big[\,\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}\ \pm\ 1.96\,\widehat{\mathrm{se}}\,\Big].

13

\ast
Note.
With an unpenalized intercept,
H\mathbf{1}_{n}=\mathbf{1}_{n}
, hence
b=\mathbf{0}
and
c=a
.

Algorithm 7

95% Wald confidence interval for
\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{sf}}_{\mathrm{PPI}}
with variance correction (KRR + unpenalized intercept)

funding:
No funding received for this research.
